L nvm is a version manager for [node.js](https://nodejs.org/en/), designed to be installed per-user, and invoked per-shell. `nvm` works on any POSIX-compliant shell (sh, [dash](https://git.kernel.org/pub/scm/utils/dash/dash.git), [ksh](https://github.com/ksh93/ksh), [zsh](https://www.zsh.org), [bash](https://www.gnu.org/software/bash/)), in particular on these platforms: unix, [macOS](https://www.apple.com/macos/), and [Windows WSL](https://github.com/nvm-sh/nvm#important-notes).

L Running either of the above commands downloads a script and runs it. The script clones the nvm repository to `~/.nvm`, and attempts to add the source lines from the snippet below to the correct profile file (`~/.bashrc`, `~/.bash_profile`, `~/.zshrc`, or `~/.profile`). If you find the install script is updating the wrong profile file, set the `$PROFILE` env var to the profile file’s path, and then rerun the installation script.

L - You can instruct the installer to not edit your shell config (for example if you already get completions via a [zsh nvm plugin](https://github.com/ohmyzsh/ohmyzsh/tree/master/plugins/nvm)) by setting `PROFILE=/dev/null` before running the `install.sh` script. Here's an example one-line command to do that: `PROFILE=/dev/null bash -c 'curl -o- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nvm-sh/nvm/v0.40.8/install.sh | bash'`

L #### Installing in Docker

L 
L When invoking bash as a non-interactive shell, like in a [Docker](https://www.docker.com) container, none of the regular profile files are sourced. In order to use `nvm`, `node`, and [`npm`](https://www.npmjs.com) like normal, you can instead specify the special `BASH_ENV` variable, which bash sources when invoked non-interactively.

L 
L # Create a script file sourced by both interactive and non-interactive bash shells
T 82 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L ENV BASH_ENV="${HOME:-/root}/.bash_env"
T 39 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L RUN touch "${BASH_ENV}"
T 23 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L RUN echo '. "${BASH_ENV}"' >> ~/.bashrc
T 39 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L # Download and install nvm
T 26 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L RUN curl -o- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nvm-sh/nvm/v0.40.8/install.sh | PROFILE="${BASH_ENV}" bash
T 105 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown

L More robust, works in CI/CD-Jobs. Can be run in interactive and non-interactive containers.

L See https://github.com/nvm-sh/nvm/issues/3531.

L ```Dockerfile
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown punctuation.definition.markdown
T 13 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown fenced_code.block.language
L FROM ubuntu:latest
T 18 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L ARG NODE_VERSION=20
T 19 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L # install curl
T 14 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L RUN apt update && apt install curl -y
T 37 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L # install nvm
T 13 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L RUN curl -o- https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nvm-sh/nvm/v0.40.8/install.sh | bash
T 83 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L # set env
T 9 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L ENV NVM_DIR="${HOME:-/root}/.nvm"
T 33 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L # install node
T 14 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L RUN bash -c "source $NVM_DIR/nvm.sh && nvm install $NODE_VERSION"
T 65 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L # set ENTRYPOINT for reloading nvm-environment
T 46 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L ENTRYPOINT ["bash", "-c", "source $NVM_DIR/nvm.sh && exec \"$@\"", "--"]
T 72 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L # set cmd to bash
T 17 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L CMD ["/bin/bash"]
T 17 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L ```

L Since OS X 10.9, `/usr/bin/git` has been preset by [Xcode](https://developer.apple.com/xcode/) command line tools, which means we can't properly detect if Git is installed or not. You need to manually install the Xcode command line tools before running the install script, otherwise, it'll fail. (see [#1782](https://github.com/nvm-sh/nvm/issues/1782))

L **Note** For Macs with the Apple Silicon chip, node started offering **arm64** arch Darwin packages since v16.0.0 and experimental **arm64** support when compiling from source since v14.17.0. If you are facing issues installing node using `nvm`, you may want to update to one of those versions or later.

T 42 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L ```sh
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown punctuation.definition.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown fenced_code.block.language.markdown
L command -v nvm
T 7 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown meta.embedded.block.shellscript meta.statement.shell meta.statement.command.shell meta.statement.command.name.shell entity.name.function.call.shell entity.name.command.shell support.function.builtin.shell
T 8 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown meta.embedded.block.shellscript meta.statement.shell meta.statement.command.shell
T 9 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown meta.embedded.block.shellscript meta.statement.shell meta.statement.command.shell string.unquoted.argument.shell constant.other.option.dash.shell
T 10 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown meta.embedded.block.shellscript meta.statement.shell meta.statement.command.shell string.unquoted.argument constant.other.option
T 11 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown meta.embedded.block.shellscript meta.statement.shell meta.statement.command.shell meta.argument.shell
T 14 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown meta.embedded.block.shellscript meta.statement.shell meta.statement.command.shell meta.argument.shell string.unquoted.argument.shell
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown punctuation.definition.markdown

L which should output `nvm` if the installation was successful. Please note that `which nvm` will not work, since `nvm` is a sourced shell function, not an executable binary.

L If you're running a system without prepackaged binary available, which means you're going to install node or [io.js](https://iojs.org) from its source code, you need to make sure your system has a C++ compiler. For OS X, Xcode will work, for [Debian](https://www.debian.org)/[Ubuntu](https://ubuntu.com) based GNU/Linux, the `build-essential` and `libssl-dev` packages work.

L **Note:** Git versions before v1.7 may face a problem of cloning `nvm` source from [GitHub](https://github.com) via https protocol, and there is also different behavior of git before v1.6, and git prior to [v1.17.10](https://github.com/git/git/commit/5a7d5b683f869d3e3884a89775241afa515da9e7) can not clone tags, so the minimum required git version is v1.7.10. If you are interested in the problem we mentioned here, please refer to GitHub's [HTTPS cloning errors](https://help.github.com/articles/https-cloning-errors/) article.

L   - `current`: the version currently active in this shell (i.e. what `node` resolves to via `$PATH`). It is **not** affected by `.nvmrc`. Useful when you want to refer to the active version explicitly &mdash; e.g. `nvm which current` always prints the path to the active `node`, regardless of whether an `.nvmrc` file is present.

L ### Long-term Support
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.3.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L Node has a [schedule](https://github.com/nodejs/Release#release-schedule) for long-term support (LTS) You can reference LTS versions in aliases and `.nvmrc` files with the notation `lts/*` for the latest LTS, and `lts/argon` for LTS releases from the "argon" line, for example. In addition, the following commands support LTS arguments:

L Any time your local copy of `nvm` connects to https://nodejs.org, it will re-create the appropriate local aliases for all available LTS lines. These aliases (stored under `$NVM_DIR/alias/lts`), are managed by `nvm`, and you should not modify, remove, or create these files - expect your changes to be undone, and expect meddling with these files to cause bugs that will likely not be supported.

L The header is only sent with requests for URLs under a mirror, never to any other host. If exactly one of `$NVM_NODEJS_ORG_MIRROR` and `$NVM_IOJS_ORG_MIRROR` is set, only that mirror receives it; if both are set, both do; if neither is, the default mirrors (`https://nodejs.org/dist` and `https://iojs.org/dist`) do.

L 
L `curl` 7.58.0 and later, which nvm prefers, drops the header when the mirror redirects to another host. When only `wget` is available, nvm makes GNU Wget follow a redirect only if it stays on the same origin (scheme, host, and port), and fails on one that does not, since `wget` would send the header there too; use `curl` with a mirror that hands downloads off to another host. Other `wget`s, like BusyBox's, cannot be kept from following redirects, and send the header wherever the mirror redirects.

L nodejs.org does not publish a binary for every platform and architecture nvm can detect. Where it does not, `nvm install` falls back by default to compiling from source, which is slow and needs a C++ toolchain (see [Important Notes](#important-notes)); set `$NVM_NO_SOURCE_FALLBACK` to `1` to make a missing binary an error instead.

L Neither host is gapless, and both change over time; the `index.tab` at the root of each is authoritative. Where neither has a binary, `nvm install` compiles from source, except on non-WSL Windows, which nvm cannot build on; on FreeBSD and OpenBSD it skips the download and goes straight to source. Alpine has its own section: [Installing nvm on Alpine Linux](#installing-nvm-on-alpine-linux).

L 
L You can create a `.nvmrc` file containing a node version number (or any other string that `nvm` understands; see `nvm --help` for details) in the project root directory (or any parent directory).
T 17 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 18 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 24 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown

T 195 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L Afterwards, `nvm use`, `nvm install`, and `nvm which` will use the version specified in the `.nvmrc` file if no version is supplied on the command line; if no `.nvmrc` is found either, they exit with status `127`. (`nvm exec` and `nvm run` follow the same `.nvmrc` lookup, but currently fall back to the active node if neither resolves &mdash; treat that fallback as undefined behavior; pass an explicit version if you need predictable scripting.) If you want the currently active version, pass `current` explicitly (e.g. `nvm which current`) &mdash; `current` is not affected by `.nvmrc`.

L `nvm use` et. al. will traverse directory structure upwards from the current directory looking for the `.nvmrc` file. In other words, running `nvm use` et. al. in any subdirectory of a directory with an `.nvmrc` will result in that `.nvmrc` being utilized.

L 
L The contents of a `.nvmrc` file **must** contain precisely one `<version>` (as described by `nvm --help`) followed by a newline. `.nvmrc` files may also have comments. The comment delimiter is `#`, and it and any text after it, as well as blank lines, and leading and trailing white space, will be ignored when parsing. Any other control character (besides a tab) makes the file invalid.

L ## Running Tests
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown

L 
L Tests are written in [Urchin]. Install Urchin (and other dependencies) like so:
T 21 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 22 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown meta.link.reference.markdown punctuation.definition.link.title.begin.markdown
T 28 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown meta.link.reference.markdown string.other.link.title.markdown
T 29 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown meta.link.reference.markdown punctuation.definition.link.title.end.markdown
T 79 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L     npm install
T 4 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 15 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L There are slow tests and fast tests. The slow tests do things like install node
T 79 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L and check that the right versions are used. The fast tests fake this to test
T 76 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L things like aliases and uninstalling. From the root of the nvm git repository,
T 78 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L run the fast tests like this:
T 29 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L     npm run test/fast
T 4 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L Run the slow tests like this:
T 29 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L     npm run test/slow
T 4 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L Run all of the tests like this:
T 31 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L     npm test
T 4 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 12 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L `npm run test/fast` runs `make` inside a clean environment (`env -i ... bash -lc`), so `node` must be on the `PATH` of a fresh login `bash` shell.

L ## Environment variables

L nvm exposes the following environment variables:
T 48 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L - `NVM_DIR` - nvm's installation directory. If it is not set when `nvm.sh` is sourced, bash, zsh, and ksh93 use the directory containing `nvm.sh`; other shells (like dash) cannot tell where a sourced file lives, so they use the default install location (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/nvm` when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is set, otherwise `~/.nvm`). If nvm is installed elsewhere, set `NVM_DIR` before sourcing `nvm.sh`.

L ## Compatibility Issues
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown

L `nvm` will encounter some issues if you have some non-default settings set. (see [#606](https://github.com/nvm-sh/nvm/issues/606))
T 1 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown
T 4 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown
T 5 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown

T 86 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown meta.link.inline.markdown string.other.link.title.markdown
T 87 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown meta.link.inline.markdown punctuation.definition.link.title.end.markdown
T 88 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown meta.link.inline.markdown punctuation.definition.metadata.markdown

T 129 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown meta.link.inline.markdown punctuation.definition.metadata.markdown

L ## Installing nvm on Alpine Linux
T 2 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown punctuation.definition.heading.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown
T 33 text.html.markdown markup.heading.markdown heading.2.markdown entity.name.section.markdown
L 
L In order to provide the best performance (and other optimizations), nvm will download and install pre-compiled binaries for Node (and npm) when you run `nvm install X`. The Node project compiles, tests and hosts/provides these pre-compiled binaries which are built for mainstream/traditional Linux distributions (such as Debian, Ubuntu, [CentOS](https://www.centos.org), [RedHat](https://www.redhat.com) et al).

T 411 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L [Alpine Linux](https://www.alpinelinux.org), unlike mainstream/traditional Linux distributions, is based on [BusyBox](https://www.busybox.net/), a very compact (~5MB) Linux distribution. BusyBox (and thus Alpine Linux) uses a different C/C++ stack to most mainstream/traditional Linux distributions - [musl](https://www.musl-libc.org/). This makes binary programs built for such mainstream/traditional distributions incompatible with Alpine Linux, so on `x64` and `arm64` nvm requests a `musl` build instead. nodejs.org publishes those only for some recent `x64` releases, so for anything else the download 404s and `nvm install X` falls back to compiling from source, unless you point it at a mirror that has one (see [Platforms without official binaries](#platforms-without-official-binaries)).

L _Note: Alpine 3.5 can only install NodeJS versions up to v6.9.5, Alpine 3.6 can only install versions up to v6.10.3, Alpine 3.7 installs versions up to v8.9.3, Alpine 3.8 installs versions up to v8.14.0, Alpine 3.9 installs versions up to v10.19.0, Alpine 3.10 installs versions up to v10.24.1, Alpine 3.11 installs versions up to v12.22.6, Alpine 3.12 installs versions up to v12.22.12, Alpine 3.13 & 3.14 install versions up to v14.20.0, Alpine 3.15 & 3.16 install versions up to v16.16.0 (**These are all versions on the main branch**). Alpine 3.5 - 3.12 required the package [`python2`](https://www.python.org/) to build NodeJS, as they are older versions to build. Alpine 3.13+ requires `python3` to successfully build newer NodeJS versions, but you can use `python2` with Alpine 3.13+ if you need to build versions of node supported in Alpine 3.5 - 3.15, you just need to specify what version of NodeJS you need to install in the package install script._

L 
L **Macs with Apple Silicon chips**
T 2 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.bold.markdown punctuation.definition.bold.markdown
T 31 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.bold.markdown
T 33 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.bold.markdown punctuation.definition.bold.markdown
L 
L Experimental support for the Apple Silicon chip architecture was added in node.js v15.3 and full support was added in v16.0.
T 124 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L Because of this, if you try to install older versions of node as usual, you will probably experience either compilation errors when installing node or out-of-memory errors while running your code.
T 196 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
L 
L So, if you want to run a version prior to v16.0 on an Apple Silicon Mac, it may be best to compile node targeting the `x86_64` Intel architecture so that [Rosetta 2](https://support.apple.com/en-us/HT211861) can translate the `x86_64` processor instructions to ARM-based Apple Silicon instructions.
T 118 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown
T 119 text.html.markdown meta.paragraph.markdown markup.inline.raw.string.markdown punctuation.definition.raw.markdown

L 
L This deletes your `resolv.conf` file that is automatically generated when you run WSL, creates a new file and puts `nameserver 8.8.8.8`, then creates a `wsl.conf` file and adds `[network]` and `generateResolveConf = false` to prevent auto-generation of that file.
